## One-Brain Coaching Engine — ARCHITECTURE LOCKED, build sequence (owner-gated promotion to active)

**GATE A status (2026-07-11) — progression EXTEND on evidence provenance, NOT engine quality.** Owner live review of the production `Brain_Shadow` window: 1,679 rows / 1,288 progression / **852 comparable progression decisions, 852/852 within tolerance** (max weight delta 5 lb, max rep delta 1), **zero orchestrator/assembly errors, zero `ok:false`**. So the Brain's raw progression performance **passes** the §5 tolerance review. **BUT** the window mixes genuine athlete activity with automated probes/simulations/canaries/Playwright/smoke (hourly clusters of 100–190 events; only ~1 confirmed real logged workout), and the rows lacked provenance to count the 50-real-event floor — so **GATE A remains EXTEND**.
- ✅ **PR-GATEA2 — read-only eligible-window scorecard (SHIPPED).** Pure `services/gateAScorecard.js` + read-only CLI `scripts/gate-a-scorecard.js`. Counts an event toward the floor ONLY when `evidence_class === 'athlete_ui' ∧ evidence_eligible === TRUE` (fail-closed; old/unknown/synthetic/malformed/missing-provenance excluded and reported by class + reason; never infers eligibility from timestamps/routes/volume). Per-decision-type (never averaged): progression metrics (floor ≥50 / preferred ≥100, variety, comparable, within/out tolerance with region-aware ±5/±10/±1 and a review bucket for undeterminable region, max deltas, agreement, ok:false-by-reason, orchestrator/assembly-error automatic blocker, confidence tiers, latency, skipped, route + planning-vs-in-workout distribution); `workout` → **INELIGIBLE** (no block-level divergence metric); intent routing → **SEPARATE** (Intent_Shadow lane). **Safety criteria are never auto-passed from numeric columns** — zero-unsafe-coaching is `requires_human_review` (never "0 errors ⇒ 0 unsafe"), plus a representative-review section. CLI requires explicit `--env=production|sandbox`, prints the workbook/tab it reads, fails closed on env mismatch or a missing provenance header, warns on stale pre-provenance rows, writes **no** Sheet data, prints no secrets. Tests: `test/gateAScorecard.test.js` + `test/gateAScorecardCli.test.js` (red-first). Does NOT promote — necessary, not sufficient, for the owner's §8 decision.
  - **Follow-up `[housekeeping]` (deferred, non-blocking review note):** `computeScorecard` validates the provenance column *names* are present in the header but then reads every cell *positionally* against the frozen `COL` map — a header with the right names in a different order would pass validation yet misparse. Acceptable today under Brain_Shadow's documented append-only "read positionally" contract (the sole writer `brainShadow.js _persistRow` emits the frozen 19-col order, and reordering is already a schema-migration violation). A future fail-closed hardening could assert header *order*, not just name presence, so a non-frozen tab reader never inherits the positional assumption silently. Left out of PR-GATEA2 to keep it one-concern.
- ⛔ **PR-A5 (promote progression) remains blocked** until a **fresh eligible window** (≥50 `athlete_ui` events, varied) is collected after the GATEA1 deploy + window reset and clears every §5 bar. The pre-GATEA1 window is archived as engineering evidence and does not count. No bar was weakened — the floor was made countable.

Blueprint: [`docs/COACHING_ENGINE_ARCHITECTURE.md`](./docs/COACHING_ENGINE_ARCHITECTURE.md). Contracts spec: [`docs/COACHING_CONTRACTS_SPEC.md`](./docs/COACHING_CONTRACTS_SPEC.md). Owner direction (2026-06-30): **Atlas has ONE Brain.** Every surface (button, chat, voice, wearable, calendar, push, API, future desktop) expresses *intent*; the Brain owns every coaching decision and number; the LLM only words decisions. The UI never determines intelligence. This section captures the build so it is not re-derived from chat. **Captured + sequenced, not promoted to `docs/ACTIVE_ROADMAP.md`** — promotion (reordering ahead of the Trust-Critical Coach Interaction Layer) is an owner priority call. Two prerequisites are owner-gated (below).

**Why this exists:** the analytics-vs-Brian audit (2026-06-30) found Atlas at risk of two competing recommendation engines. Resolution: the Orchestrator is always the single decider; `analytics.js` is delegated-to only for un-migrated decisions, shrinking each step until it dies. Never two engines racing. The Brain stays pure and read-only — the existing preview→approve→write trust loop is untouched.

**Build sequence (each a tiny PR; deterministic-engine-first; no LLM/write/schema change unless flagged):**

1. ✅ **Contracts — `IntentEnvelope`** (shipped) — `config/coaching/contracts/intent.vocabulary.json` (intent + source + constraint-key schema, data-first single source of truth) + `services/intentEnvelope.js` (`buildIntentEnvelope`/`validateIntentEnvelope`, pure; validator is total — never throws, never mutates). 56 tests (`test/intentEnvelope.test.js`): five canonical examples, all 13 intents, every validation rule (§1.4), constraint enum/range checks, the extraction biconditional, system-actor rule, intent-specific required constraints, purity + lane-equivalence. No I/O at call time, no LLM, no write path, no route.
2. ✅ **Contracts — `CapabilityManifest`** (shipped) — `config/coaching/manifests/intent-capabilities.json` (all 13 intents + `brain`/`routes_to`/`decision_type`/`read_only`) + `capabilities.json` (19 capability descriptors with status mirroring the audit) + `services/capabilityManifest.js` (`validateManifest`, `resolve` = transitive `depends_on` closure + deterministic topo-sort, lookups). 21 tests (`test/capabilityManifest.test.js`): manifest integrity (every intent has an entry, every capability/depends_on resolves, requires/optional ∈ vocabulary, decision_types legal, read_only→progress_readout), resolver closure + dependency ordering + missing-flagging, and the **orchestrator-is-rule-free guard** (the reader imports only `node:` builtins; Brain modules referenced only as data strings). Spec §2 reconciled in the same PR (three self-consistency fixes: `produces` is an output namespace not validated against the input vocabulary; added `constraint.exclude_exercises`; resolver does closure not manual transitive listing; added `brain:false`/`routes_to` for non-Brain intents like `log_intent`). No I/O at call time beyond the static JSON, no LLM, no write path, no route.
3. ✅ **Contracts — `CoachingDecision`** (shipped) — `config/coaching/contracts/decision.contract.json` (decision_type/status/tier/action/safety enums, caveat_keys = the exact values `confidenceModule` emits, per-decision_type payload registry with prescribed-number fields) + `services/coachingDecision.js` (`buildCoachingDecision`/`validateCoachingDecision`, total/pure). Enforces all of spec §3.4: envelope+enums, discriminator integrity, the ask/answer four-way lockstep, the **trust-contract invariant** (every prescribed number in `payload` must appear as a value in `explanation_inputs`), safety escalation (`blocking ⟹ action≠act` + `safety_flag_present` caveat), progress_readout-no-prescription, provenance disjoint+⊆-plan, and missing_info validity+ordering. 35 tests (`test/coachingDecision.test.js`) + `test/contracts-integrity.test.js` (10 tests, the one-Brain guard: every vocabulary intent has a manifest entry and no orphans, manifest↔decision decision_types agree, **caveat enum has not drifted from `confidenceModule`**, complete/partial capabilities point at real files & missing ones do not, fixtures validate). Spec §3 reconciled (caveat values lowercased to match the producer; rule 7 split into decision-validator ⊆ vs orchestrator =). Also tightened `IntentEnvelope.asOf` to strict ISO-8601 (resolves the follow-up below). No I/O at call time beyond the static JSON, no LLM, no write path, no route.
4. ✅ **State Assembly spec + module** (shipped) — the read-model the manifests' input keys draw from (hydrate logs/profile/memory/deload-state once; quarantine all Sheets I/O; keep the Brain pure). Spec: `docs/COACHING_STATE_ASSEMBLY_SPEC.md`. Module: `services/stateAssembly.js` — `assembleState({ readers?, asOf })` (read-only; injected readers default to `trainingStore.getLogRows` + `deloadState.readCurrentDeloadState` + a `getProfileGoal`-backed profile reader, all **lazy-required inside `_defaultReaders` wrapped in try/catch** so importing the module — and any stub-injecting test — never pulls `sheets.js`/`googleapis`, and a reader-layer load failure degrades to an empty snapshot, never a 500) + `knownKeys(snapshot, envelope)` for the missing-info handshake. `memory_snapshot`/`bodyweight_history` are derived from `log_history` (12-col positional rows), not separately read; `equipment_profile` null until a durable source exists. 18 tests (`test/stateAssembly.test.js`, all stub-injected — no live Sheets): snapshot shape, derived keys, `knownKeys`, graceful degradation (throwing/empty/missing readers, all-fail, garbage params), purity, and the read-only guard (no writer call; top-level requires are pure derivers only). No schema/LLM/write change.
5. **Orchestrator + `hybrid` shadow attach** `[correctness]` — split into PR-5a (pure module) + PR-5b (gated `index.js` attach) to isolate the high-risk file touch.
   - ✅ **PR-5b — gated `index.js` hybrid attach** (shipped) — `services/coachRunners.js` (pure adapters: `safety`→traffic-light, `confidence`→ask-vs-act, shaping Brian-module outputs into orchestrator decision fragments; defensive, unit-tested) + a tiny gated block on `GET /api/recommend/next/:liftCode`: when `process.env.ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE === 'hybrid'`, build a `progression_review` IntentEnvelope, `assembleState` (real readers), `orchestrate` with `buildRunners()`, and attach the result as `recommendation.brian` **only if `validateCoachingDecision` passes** — all inside its own try/catch that can NEVER alter or fail the response. Default (unset/`legacy`) = byte-identical response, no `brian` field. No write path / `test_mode` / proof-field / trust-loop / slash-notation / undo touch. 11 tests (`test/coachRunners.test.js`): adapters + the full shadow composition (envelope → assembleState(stub) → orchestrate → validate) mirroring the index.js path (no live Sheets); CI api-smoke confirms the legacy endpoint is unchanged. Feature flag remains three-state `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE=legacy|hybrid|brian`. Also softened the orchestrator docstring to "assembled (not validated)" per PR #754 review; the index.js validate-before-surface guard is that note's fix.
6. ✅ **Keystone #1 — Scenario Classifier** (shipped) — `services/scenarioClassifier.js`: `classifyScenario(params)` → `{ scenario_id, rationale }` | null. Maps a lift's derived state (userState e1RM trend + sessionsTracked, expectedPerformance plateau, readiness tier, injury, overperforming) to one of the six `progression.rules.json` scenarios with **safety-first precedence** (injury > plateau > fatigue[low-readiness/declining] > progression state[overperforming→underloaded / improving→on_target / stalling→normal_variability]). Every produced `scenario_id` is accepted by `progressionModule.recommendProgression` (test-verified) — closes the keystone gap that left progression un-drivable. Pure, no I/O/LLM/write. Manifest `scenario_classifier` status flipped `missing`→`partial` (required in the same PR: the file now exists, so the contracts-integrity guard demands the non-missing status; the manifest test was updated to reference `session_generator` as the remaining missing keystone). 19 tests (`test/scenarioClassifier.test.js`): id-alignment drift guard vs the rules config, each branch, precedence, guards, and the progressionModule-accepts-every-id check.
   - ✅ **Follow-up — wire scenario_classifier + progression runner adapters** (shipped) — `services/coachRunners.js` gained `scenario_classifier` (derives userState liftState + plateau from the snapshot → `classifyScenario`) and `progression` (reads the scenario from `ctx.results`, builds context from the last non-warmup working set → `recommendProgression`, emits a `progression` decision fragment with numbers echoed under matching keys for the key-aware trust contract) adapters, plus a shared `_deriveLiftState` used by both and the confidence runner (so confidence rises above `ask` on real history). Result: `progression_review` now yields an **answered** progression decision on rich history (the first real Brian answer on the shadow path) and an honest `clarification` on thin history. 19 tests (`test/coachRunners.test.js`).
   - **Follow-up (before enabling `hybrid`) — avoid the double Sheets read** `[housekeeping]` — the index.js hybrid attach calls `assembleState({ asOf })` with default readers, re-reading `Log_Cleaned` even though `allLog` is already loaded on that request. Thread the fetched rows through via injected readers (over `allLog` + the real deload/profile readers so the snapshot stays complete) before `hybrid` is switched on. Harmless while the flag is off. Reviewer note PR #755 (claude[bot]).
7. ✅ **Keystone #2 — Session Generator** (module shipped) — `services/sessionGenerator.js`: `buildSession(state, constraints)` → `{ payload, explanation_inputs, why_today, substitutions_applied, notes }` | null. Composes shipped modules: focus→movement patterns (reusing `onboardingSessionPlan` PATTERN_VARIANTS), equipment/exclusion filtering, per-lift prescription via the new shared `services/liftPrescription.js` (`prescribeLift` = deriveLiftState + plateau → classifyScenario → recommendProgression; chronological last-working-set), injury→drop-affected-patterns (substitutions recorded), duration fitting (trim accessories), and the deload protocol (`deloadProtocols.computePrescription`) when `deload_state` active. Per-block key-aware `explanation_inputs.blocks[i]` kept in lockstep. Pure — no I/O/LLM/write. Manifest `session_generator` status flipped missing→partial (both keystones now partial; `capabilityManifest` test updated to reference `equipment` as the remaining missing capability). 12 tests (`test/sessionGenerator.test.js`): validity via `validateCoachingDecision` (workout + trust contract), pattern coverage, equipment filtering, injury drop, duration fitting, deload load-cut, and an un-echoed number failing validation. **v1 limitations (follow-ups):** `sets`/non-deload `target_rir` are prescription defaults (derive from goal/scenario); volume surfaced as `why_today` facts (full MRV-capping deferred); history-backed lifts only (cold-start lifts noted, deferred to `onboardingSessionPlan`).
   - ✅ **Follow-up — session_generator runner adapter** (shipped) — `coachRunners.sessionGeneratorRunner` calls `buildSession(snapshot, envelope.constraints)` → `{ decision: { payload, explanation_inputs } }`; registered in `buildRunners()`. `best_workout` now produces an **answered `workout`** decision on rich full-body history (the first full Brian workout) and clarification on no history. The confidence runner falls back to the **most-trained lift** as the representative signal when the intent names no `target_lift` (so a whole-workout intent can clear `ask`). Also normalized `sessionGenerator` equipment casing (PR #759 review) so mixed-case constraints match `PATTERN_VARIANTS`. 25 tests in `test/coachRunners.test.js`.
   - ✅ **Follow-up — dedupe coachRunners onto `liftPrescription.js`** (shipped) — `coachRunners.js` now imports `deriveLiftState`/`derivePlateau`/`lastWorkingSet` from `services/liftPrescription.js` and its private `_normRow`/`_isWarmup`/`_liftRows`/`_deriveLiftState`/`_derivePlateau`/`_lastWorkingSet` copies were removed (~90 lines). Behavior identical — all coachRunners/orchestrator/sessionGenerator/contracts tests pass unchanged.
   - ✅ **Coach's Pick promotion** (owner-approved 2026-07-02 — "We can work on gated stuff that's fine"; shipped same day) — `GET /api/plan/intent-recommendation` now runs the same staged `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE` gate as the two prior promotions: `hybrid` = shadow attach (`result.brian`, validated `best_workout` decision; response otherwise byte-identical), `brian` = an answered workout decision's blocks REPLACE the recommended intent's exercise list via `planBrianPickOverride`/`applyBrianPickOverride` (numbers verbatim; labels/why_today/other intents/todays_read stay legacy; ineligible → untouched legacy + `engine_source.reason`). No separate deload guard needed — `buildSession` is itself deload-aware. Runs before the barbell-loadability pass so that display rule applies uniformly to both engines. `legacy` default remains byte-identical (route-tested). `scoreIntents` itself is NOT deleted — it still produces the surface (labels, why, other intents) and the fallback; its retirement is a later evidence-driven step. Review #813 note (`[polish]`, before flipping `brian` on for real): the override keeps legacy `why_today` prose, which can read stale against a Brian-swapped list — if live testing shows the incongruity, derive it from `brian.payload.why_today` (already emitted as machine facts) worded by the deterministic templates.
     - **Follow-up — manifest required-vs-optional/defaulted capability semantics** `[correctness]` — the serve rail is a decision-type allowlist, NOT the final capability-manifest design. The real promotion blockers for Coach's Pick are fatigue/volume/equipment *semantics* and runner completeness (equipment/movement_patterns are `missing`; fatigue/user_state/scenario_classifier/progression/session_generator are `partial`), but `capabilities.json` cannot yet express *which* skipped/defaulted capabilities are REQUIRED for a serve-safe decision vs merely optional/defaulted. Design a manifest expression of required-vs-optional/defaulted capability status + a resolver-level serve-readiness check, then replace the coarse `SERVE_ELIGIBLE_DECISION_TYPES` allowlist with it. Flipping `workout` to serve-eligible is an owner-gated promotion decision that depends on this.
     - **Follow-up — restore direct coverage of the Coach's Pick block→exercise mapper** `[test-coverage]` (PR #849 review note, non-blocking) — with the serve rail up, `planBrianPickOverride` short-circuits at `not_serve_eligible` for `workout`, so its `prescribed.map(...)` transform (`services/coachEnginePromotion.js`, reps→target_reps / scenario_id→reason) is unreachable through the public function and no longer directly asserted (`applyBrianPickOverride` is now exercised via a literal `PICK_PLAN`). A regression in that mapper would be silent until `workout` is promoted. When promoting (or if the mapper is refactored sooner), pin it directly — extract the map to a small exported pure helper and assert it, or restore the "maps every prescribed block verbatim" assertion once the rail lets `workout` through. Deliberately NOT solved here: adding a test-only production export now would expand this focused safety-rail PR.
   - ✅ **Constraint Resolver — pure module** (shipped 2026-07-02; composer-first Phase 0 item 3 / architecture item 3) — `services/constraintResolver.js`: `resolveConstraints({ stored, request })` → `{ constraints, applied, ignored }`. Closes the verified gap: the `Constraints` tab (typed owner-approved rules) was read by coach chat for prose context but never reached the Brain — `stateAssembly` doesn't read it, so `sessionGenerator.buildSession` only ever saw per-request constraints. The resolver maps stored rows to the IntentEnvelope constraint vocabulary (injury body-area synonyms → the `injury` enum; equipment synonyms narrow the available `equipment` list; preference rows → `exclude_exercises`) and merges with request constraints under safety-first precedence: injuries additive across every rule verb, request = present-tense truth for circumstances (equipment intersect; hard conflict → request wins, recorded), nothing unmappable is silently dropped (`ignored` carries a reason). Pure/total, vocabulary JSON as the single enum source. Manifest `constraint_resolver` flipped missing→partial. 18 tests (`test/constraintResolver.test.js`) incl. envelope-validation alignment and applied∪ignored=stored provenance.
     - **Follow-up — multiple-injury session shaping** `[correctness]` — the constraint contract's `injury` key is a single enum, so the resolver surfaces extra mapped injuries as `ignored: single_injury_slot_v1`. Extend the vocabulary/`sessionGenerator` to an additive `injuries[]` (drop the union of affected patterns), then delete the slot limit. PR #809 review note to fix alongside: a non-winning injury row currently appears in BOTH `applied` (it mapped) and `ignored` (lost the slot) — make the two lists disjoint when the slot limit goes.
     - **Follow-up — `rule=limit` semantics** `[polish]` — avoid/substitute resolve today; `limit` rows are surfaced-not-applied (`limit_not_resolvable_v1`). Define the engine meaning (volume cap? frequency cap?) before mapping it.

**"Before enabling `hybrid`" hardening cluster** (all gated-off, no exposure today; do before flipping `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE=hybrid` on for real observation):
- ✅ **Server-side coach-engine shadow observability** (owner-approved 2026-07-05, ring-only phase; shipped same day) — closes the Hybrid Shadow Readiness audit's two BLOCKERS (no server-side capture of Brian decisions; no divergence metric). New `services/coachShadow.js` mirrors `services/intentShadow.js`: a capped in-memory ring (`RING_MAX=50`) + one `[coach-shadow]` console line, served read-only by auth-gated `GET /api/debug/coach-shadow` (ring + basic aggregates: by_route/mode/driven_by, comparable/diverged, avg_ms). `observeBrianDecision(...)` is TOTAL (never throws) and wired into all three gate blocks (`/api/plan/today`, `/api/plan/intent-recommendation`, `/api/recommend/next`) after a validated decision, in BOTH hybrid and brian, inside its own try/catch — observation can never alter or fail the served response. `computeDivergence()` compares ONLY Brian-prescribed (non-null) fields so intentionally-null values (sets/target_rir) are not false divergence. Per-decision Brain elapsed `ms` captured. **Client attach trimmed:** in hybrid the wire `.brian` is now `summarizeBrianDecision()` (a safe flat summary: type/status, the prescription incl. trimmed workout blocks, confidence/safety TIER/LEVEL) instead of the full CoachingDecision — raw provenance/module lists/explanation_inputs no longer ship to the client. `hybridCompare.summarizeBrian` dual-reads (full decision OR trimmed summary) so the dev compare card is unaffected; brian mode still attaches the full decision (its drive-path tests read it). Tests: `test/coachShadow.test.js` (17 unit), `test/coachShadowRoute.test.js` (6 HTTP incl. the plan/today attach gap + #849 rail stays brian-serve-only while hybrid observes), `test/coachShadowDegrade.test.js` (3 HTTP: thrown-orchestrator + thrown-observation degrade across all three routes). No write path / trust-loop / proof-field / schema / Sheets-tab / production-env change. Route registered in `config/routes.js`.
    - ⚠️ **Consolidated 2026-07-05 (shadow-recorder de-dup PR)** — #850 and #851 (below) shipped two overlapping recorders. Consolidated to ONE: `brainShadow` is the sole coach-engine shadow recorder (it also captures declines/crashes, which the win-only `coachShadow` ring did not). `services/coachShadow.js` → **`services/coachDecisionSummary.js`**, reduced to just the projection helpers `summarizeBrianDecision`/`summarizeLegacyRecommendation` (the trimmed hybrid `.brian` attach + the legacy divergence input); its ring, `computeDivergence`, `observeBrianDecision`, `getCoachShadowLog`, the `/api/debug/coach-shadow` endpoint + route, and `test/coachShadowRoute.test.js`/`test/coachShadowDegrade.test.js` were removed (coverage now via `brainShadow-endpoint.test.js` + `onebrain-brian-route-drive.test.js` + `coachDecisionSummary.test.js`). The trimmed-attach behavior and `hybridCompare.summarizeBrian` dual-read are unchanged. **Follow-up:** `coachShadow` recorded `driven_by`/`reason` (did brian actually DRIVE vs fall back); `brainShadow` records `ok`/decline-`reason` but not `driven_by`. Add `driven_by` (+ a `by_driven_by` aggregate) to `observeBrainOrchestration` when the promotion bar needs the drove-vs-fell-back split.
  - ✅ **Brain ORCHESTRATOR shadow recorder — wins AND failures + durable `Brain_Shadow` tab** (shipped 2026-07-05) — the one flip blocker: hybrid now keeps a reviewable record of what the new engine *would* have said, including when it DECLINES or CRASHES. New `services/brainShadow.js` mirrors `services/intentShadow.js` (capped 50-entry ring + one `[brain-shadow]` console line + best-effort Sheets append) and is the failure-capturing sibling of `coachShadow`: where `observeBrianDecision` records only validated WINS, `observeBrainOrchestration` records EVERY orchestration at all three gates (`/api/plan/today`, `/api/plan/intent-recommendation`, `/api/recommend/next`) — `ok:true` for a valid decision (with `decision_type`/`status`/`confidence_tier`, the declared `provenance.skipped` capabilities, and legacy-vs-brian target-number divergence), and `ok:false`+reason for the paths the empty catch blocks used to swallow: `invalid_decision`, `null_decision`, `non_brain_route`, and (via `observeBrainFailure` in each catch) `orchestrator_error`/`assembly_error`. TOTAL by construction (every entry point try/caught; mode-gated to hybrid|brian; reads the served objects, never mutates them) — a shadow error can never change, delay, or fail the user-facing response. Read-only auth-gated `GET /api/debug/brain-shadow` (ring + win/fail aggregates by reason/route); registered in `config/routes.js`. `Brain_Shadow` declared in `config/sheetContract.js` optional tabs. **Durable append is a SEPARATE default-OFF opt-in** (`ATLAS_BRAIN_SHADOW_PERSIST=1`): the ring/console/endpoint are the reviewable record and are always live in hybrid|brian, but the three coach-engine READ routes stay append-free — no Sheets write, byte-identical, "do not touch write paths" honored — unless the owner turns the mirror on. Tests: `test/brainShadow.test.js` (unit: win/invalid/null/non-brain/throw, mode gate, ring cap, aggregates, default-off no-append, throwing-append TOTAL) + `test/brainShadow-endpoint.test.js` (HTTP: WIN recorded + legacy fields unchanged, a forced orchestrator throw recorded as `orchestrator_error` while the request still 200s, auth-gated endpoint, legacy inert). No write path / trust-loop / proof-field / schema / served-output change. (Widened one brittle fixed-offset source-slice assertion in `unit.test.js` that the added wiring outgrew — the barbell-loadability call still runs.)
    - **Deferred (do WITH the persist flip — harmless while default-OFF):** the `Brain_Shadow` row's `app_version` column is written by `_persistRow` but the `observeBrainOrchestration`/`observeBrainFailure` call sites in `index.js` never pass `appVersion` (unlike `observeChatMessage`, which threads `req.body.app_version`), so every persisted row's last column is blank. Wire an app-version source into the three gate call sites when `ATLAS_BRAIN_SHADOW_PERSIST` is turned on, or drop the column. (PR #851 review note.)
  - **Deferred (owner-gated, do before/with the hybrid flip):** (1) **durable shadow tab** — ✅ effectively provided by `brainShadow`'s opt-in `Brain_Shadow` tab (`ATLAS_BRAIN_SHADOW_PERSIST`); after the recorder consolidation there is no separate `Coach_Shadow` tab to build. (Owner still gates *turning the persist flag on* — it is a light nod, default-OFF.) (2) **fire-and-forget off the reply path** — today orchestrate+validate+observe are inline-awaited (adds ~2–3 Sheets reads' latency/request on three hot routes); moving the whole Brain pass off the reply path (mirroring `intentShadow`) removes that latency and the client attach entirely — a design decision for the flip. (3) **redundant `Deload_State` double-read** on `/api/plan/today` + `/api/recommend/next` (evaluateCurrentDeload + assembleState both read it) — dedupe when the flip's cost matters. (4) **sampling/rate control** (a `brainShadow` sample env) — nice-to-have for single-owner traffic. (5) **doc gaps:** `.env.example` does not document `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE` (legal `legacy|hybrid|brian`, default `legacy`); no runbook doc / `render.yaml`. (6) ✅ **promotion acceptance bar** (shipped 2026-07-05) — the permanent governance process is now [`docs/ONE_BRAIN_PROMOTION_CRITERIA.md`](./docs/ONE_BRAIN_PROMOTION_CRITERIA.md): observation-window floor (50 real events min / 100+ preferred, varied), the full acceptance checklist (zero unsafe, zero orchestrator errors, ≥90% agreement on comparable decisions within ±5 lb upper / ±10 lb lower / ±1 rep initial tolerances, explainable `ok:false`), automatic blockers, per-decision-type promotion via the serve rail, owner review, and rollback. Applies to every future Brain version. (7) **`workout` (Coach's Pick) divergence is unmeasured** `[correctness]` (PR #850 review note; post-consolidation the recorder is `brainShadow`) — `brainShadow._divergence` compares only top-level `target_weight`/`target_reps`, which a `workout` orchestration leaves null (its numbers live in `payload.blocks`). So `/api/plan/intent-recommendation` entries are always `comparable:false` and contribute nothing to the `comparable`/`diverged` aggregates — the ring still records the entry (not broken), but Coach's Pick divergence is effectively unmeasured. Before the promotion acceptance bar (item 6) leans on those counts, define a **block-level divergence** (per lift_code: weight/reps deltas vs the legacy recommended intent's exercises) for workout decisions. Deliberately NOT built here: Coach's Pick is not serve-eligible (#849), so its divergence is not yet decision-relevant, and block-level comparison is its own concern.
8. **analytics.js migration** `[housekeeping]` — move lifecycle decisions out of `analytics.js` one at a time, each deleting its inline copy, until the fused file is retired. Verified state 2026-07-03: **layoff guard** ✅ (`services/layoffGuard.js`, pre-existing) · **post-deload return** ✅ (`services/deloadPolicy.js`, pre-existing) · **in-workout just-logged anchor + effortVerdict** ✅ (shipped 2026-07-03 — `services/justLoggedAnchor.js`, byte-identical extraction; analytics re-exports both so every consumer is unchanged; `stimulusGovernor` repointed to the new module). **staleness guard** ✅ (shipped 2026-07-03 — `services/stalenessGuard.js`, `applyStalenessGuard(current, { daysSinceLastSession, isBodyweight, lastSet })`, byte-identical pure transform over the in-flight recommendation state; 5 module tests). **bodyweight rep-progression** ✅ (shipped 2026-07-03 — `services/bodyweightProgression.js`, byte-identical pure extraction; 4 module tests). **All four named lifecycle decisions are now out of the fused file.** Remaining: retirement itself, which is blocked on the big consumers (`scoreIntents`, `recommendNextSet`) being replaced by the promoted engine surfaces — an evidence-driven step after live `brian`-mode observation, not a mechanical one. Also filed here (PR #814 review): `computeMuscleGroupVolume` anchors its cutoff on the real `new Date()` with no injectable `today` — the durable fix for the time-bomb class is an injectable reference date so tests pin the clock instead of widening windows; do it when that function's decision migrates.
9. ✅ **Hybrid Coach Compare v1 — dev-only evaluation UI** (shipped, PR #766) — Settings → Debug → "Hybrid Coach Compare (dev)" card lets the owner fetch one lift's `/api/recommend/next/:liftCode` and, only when `hybridCompare.shouldShowCompareCard(recommendation)` finds a validated `recommendation.brian` attached, shows Legacy vs Brian summaries side by side with **Prefer Legacy / Prefer Brian / Neither-Bad** buttons + an optional note. Pure helpers in `public/hybridCompare.js` (UMD, browser + Node-testable): `shouldShowCompareCard`, `summarizeLegacy`, `summarizeBrian`, `buildComparisonEntry`, `loadComparisons`/`saveComparisonEntry`. Read-only fetch (same production endpoint); a preference selection never mutates the recommendation and only appends a feedback entry to `localStorage` (`atlas_hybrid_compare_v1`, capped at 200 entries) — no Sheets write, no `test_mode`/proof-field/trust-loop touch, no production-path behavior change (the card lives only in the dev Debug panel, not the coach surface). **Post-merge review fix (same PR):** the gate originally ANDed a separately-fetched `coachEngineMode` on top of `recommendation.brian`'s presence — a TOCTOU, since `recommendation.brian` is only ever attached server-side while already in hybrid mode, so the second check could only ever produce a false negative. Simplified to gate on `recommendation.brian` alone (and dropped the now-unneeded `/api/debug/config` fetch); also fixed a race where a second Compare click for a different lift could resolve out of order and silently overwrite the first (Compare button now disables while in flight). 22 unit tests (`test/hybridCompare.test.js`) + 4 Playwright e2e tests (`tests/e2e/hybrid-compare.spec.js`), including the button-disable regression.
   - **Follow-up — backend persistence for compare feedback** `[housekeeping]` — v1 is `localStorage`-only per the owner's scoped ask ("start with localStorage only and file backend persistence as follow-up"); no existing safe debug/feedback write path fit this data (the `/api/bug-report` → `Bug_Reports` tab is for actual bugs, not eval preferences — reusing it would misuse that tab's schema). A durable store (new sheet tab, or a small local file/DB) needs its own schema decision and is out of scope for v1. File as a future authorized PR when the owner wants comparisons to survive across devices/sessions instead of one browser's `localStorage`.
10. ✅ **One-Brain Promotion PR 1 — `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE=brian` for `/api/recommend/next/:liftCode` only** (shipped) — `services/coachEnginePromotion.js` (`planBrianOverride`/`applyBrianOverride`, pure) extends the existing gated `index.js` block: when `ATLAS_COACH_ENGINE==='brian'` and the Orchestrator returns a **valid, `answered`, `progression`** decision with at least one prescribed number, `target_weight`/`target_reps` DRIVE `next_target`/`recommendation`/`reasoning` — every number traced verbatim from `brian.payload`, nothing invented, no LLM in this route at all. `next_target.sets`/`target_rir`/every other legacy field is left untouched (compatibility fallback). Any error, invalid decision, `needs_clarification`, or **an active deload** (the predefined protocol always wins — a progression decision has no deload awareness) falls back to the untouched legacy response. `recommendation.engine_source = { mode:'brian', driven_by:'brian'|'legacy', reason? }` records which path produced the response; this field never appears in `legacy`/`hybrid` mode, which stay byte-identical to before. Scope: this one route only — today's pick, coach chat/message, and in-session set reaction are untouched, and no write path/Sheets schema/trust-loop was touched. 21 tests (`test/coachEnginePromotion.test.js`: every `planBrianOverride` branch including the deload-always-wins guard, plus composition tests against the **real Orchestrator** — rich history → answered → eligible with Brian's own numbers verbatim; thin history → clarification → ineligible, recommendation untouched; rich history + active deload → still ineligible) + 5 route-level tests (`test/api-smoke.test.js`: legacy byte-equivalent, hybrid shadow-only/byte-identical to legacy, brian falls back on clarification with matching metadata, brian never overrides an active deload, route stays `writeCapable:false` across all three modes).
    - ✅ **Follow-up — promote `/api/plan/today`** (shipped) — the dashboard "Today's Plan" card now runs the identical gated shadow/promotion pattern as `/api/recommend/next/:liftCode`, looped per lift already selected for the card (post-slice, so orchestration never runs on a lift the dashboard won't show); snapshot + deload state are assembled once and shared across lifts, not re-read per lift. Each lift's own try/catch, so one lift's orchestrator failure can never affect another's or fail the request. Same `recommendation.brian`/`engine_source` fields, same deload-always-wins guard, same byte-identical `legacy`/`hybrid` behavior — no new module, reuses `services/coachEnginePromotion.js` as-is. 6 route-level tests added to `test/api-smoke.test.js` (legacy byte-equivalent, hybrid shadow-only, brian per-entry fallback with metadata, brian never overrides an active deload, route stays read-only across all three modes, existing shape test). Coach chat/message (`/api/coach/message`, `/api/coach/chat`) and in-session set reaction remain unpromoted — still run `analytics.js`/`scoreIntents` only, with zero Orchestrator involvement even in `brian` mode. Each is its own future promotion PR per `docs/COACHING_ENGINE_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s "every surface hits the same Brain" table.
      **Deliberately not touched in this PR:** `GET /api/plan/intent-recommendation` (the `scoreIntents`-based single "Coach's Pick" card) — replacing that surface was separately flagged owner-gated (coach surface / product scope) earlier in this file (Session Generator keystone note above); this PR stayed on the lower-risk, already-precedented per-lift progression pattern instead.
    - ✅ **Ephemeral-set fold — the set-reaction promotion foundation** (shipped 2026-07-03) — design refined from the original filing: on this route the fresh set already arrives as query params, so the fold happens at STATE ASSEMBLY via the injected readers (`services/ephemeralSetFold.js`: `foldJustLoggedSet(rows, liftCode, set, asOf)` appends one synthetic 12-col row with the lift's sheet identity, `~ephemeral` session id sorts after any real same-day session, unfoldable input returns the rows unchanged) — NO contract-vocabulary change needed; the envelope lane (`signal` / `constraint.just_logged_set`) stays filed for future NON-API surfaces (chat/wearables) that lack a structured transport. The `just_logged_anchor` guard lifts only via `context.ephemeralSetFolded`, set in the same route block that performed the fold — consumption is provable (end-to-end test: sheet-only anchors at 200, folded anchors at the unsaved 205). In brian mode the in-workout reaction can now be Brian-driven when confidence clears; thin history still clarifies and falls back byte-identically.
    - **Follow-up — verdict/reasoning wording is a minimal deterministic template, not yet migrated to the coaching voice** `[polish]` — `brianVerdictText` in `services/coachEnginePromotion.js` produces a bare `"Brian: <action> — <weight> x <reps>."` sentence so the displayed text never contradicts the (Brian-driven) numbers next to it. This is intentionally minimal for PR 1 (no LLM, no phrase-table); a future PR could route it through the existing deterministic coach-voice templates (`public/coachVoiceTemplates.js` / `services/coachVoiceRenderer.js`) for a more natural sentence, still without inventing numbers.

**Follow-ups discovered during the build:**
- **State Assembly default `getProfile` records a spurious `'profile'` read when all fields are null** `[housekeeping]` — `_defaultReaders().getProfile` always returns a non-null object (`{ profile_goal: getProfileGoal(), training_level: null, population: null }`), so `_safeRead` records `'profile'` in `provenance.reads` even when `ATLAS_PROFILE_GOAL` is unset and every field is null — reporting a read that produced no data. Harmless today (bookkeeping), but if the orchestrator uses `provenance.reads` for `requires`-satisfaction an all-null profile would falsely count as present. Fix in **PR-5** (where `reads` is first consumed): return `null` from the default `getProfile` when `getProfileGoal()` is null. Non-blocking reviewer note from PR #752 (claude[bot]).
- **`movement_patterns` capability filename collision risk** `[housekeeping]` — the `movement_patterns` descriptor (`capabilities.json`, status `missing`) points at planned `services/movementPatternModule.js` (singular) with export `classifyPattern`. A `services/movementPatternsModule.js` (plural) already exists exporting pattern *lookup*, not classification. When the capability is built, pick a non-colliding filename (or extend the existing plural module) so the two are not confused. Non-blocking reviewer note from PR #749 (claude[bot]).

**Behavior prerequisite — Conversation Contract (philosophy, before the natural-language Intent Router):**
- **Conversation Contract v1** `[owner-gated]` — captured in [`docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md`](./docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md) (2026-07-01, owner direction). Defines *how Atlas behaves* — when it asks vs assumes, stays silent, interrupts, challenges (positively), refuses to guess, summarizes, recommends vs waits, recovers from misunderstandings, handles uncertainty — plus the voice invariants (engine owns numbers, never shame, always acknowledge a log, silence ≠ failed write, degrade gracefully, orchestrate-don't-replace). **Sequenced BEFORE the natural-language Intent Router:** the contract gives the router a consistent personality to serve; build the router first and each capability "talks" differently. Philosophy only — not prompts, not implementation. **The four behavioral dials are now owner-DECIDED (2026-07-01):** nutrition = future capability behind "guidance, not medical/dietitian replacement"; proactivity = earned/limited/user-controlled; challenge = firm-but-earned; verbosity = confirmed 11-trigger list (the owner's original ten plus #11 unexpected excellence, added in the mock-session review). Remaining owner-gated on *implementation*: the nutrition-capability build, the proactivity mechanism, and the verbosity classifier (each a future deterministic-first PR). Companion filter recorded as **Invariant I1** (`docs/INVARIANTS.md`): *the conversation is the product; every screen supports it, none competes with it.*

**Owner-gated prerequisites (reserved — do NOT build without owner approval):**
- **Input-LLM provider/model** `[owner-gated]` — the language→intent extraction boundary is a *second* LLM touchpoint = new runtime model spend + runtime-model selection (`CLAUDE.md` / `docs/OWNER_CHECKIN_RULES.md` criterion 2/3). Steps 1–6 do NOT require it (button/sensor/API use structured passthrough); chat/voice extraction does.
- **Proactivity policy** `[owner-gated]` — **policy DECIDED (owner, 2026-07-01):** initiation is *earned, limited, user-controlled* — allowed only for clear user benefit (missed workout, recovery risk, unusual fatigue, planned-session reminder, streak/save issue), no needy-app spam (see `docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md` rule 4). The *mechanism* (notification channel, user controls, frequency caps) remains a future, deterministic-first, owner-gated build — the seam stays reserved until then.

**Capability status at capture (from the audit, recorded in the blueprint):** complete — intensity, volume, plateau, deload (live), confidence, memory, goal/population, program templates, onboarding. Partial — userState (trend algo differs), progression (needs scenario), fatigue/readiness (needs check-in data), safety (substring matcher, `confidence_inversion` unbuilt). Missing — Scenario Classifier, Session Generator, Constraint Resolver, readiness-from-logs, equipment model, lifecycle decisions, nutrition (owner-gated), decision synthesizer.

## Coach Intelligence Layer — follow-ups (PR #721, scaffold)

Follow-ups filed from the automated code review of PR #721 (static data + docs only — no action needed in that PR).

- **Schema validation unenforced (no AJV)** `[housekeeping]` — `test/coachingIntelligence.test.js` uses hand-rolled structural checks rather than validating the data files against the `config/coaching/schemas/*.json` files (the repo has no JSON Schema validator). The schemas can drift silently from the actual files until a real validator runs. When the consuming module is built, add `ajv` (or equivalent) as a devDependency and replace the hand-rolled checks with full JSON Schema draft 2020-12 validation. Not urgent while the files are static data with no runtime consumer.

- **IntensityModule: Epley-derived %1RM model diverges from practitioner RPE tables at extremes** `[housekeeping]` — `services/intensityModule.js` derives %1RM from the Epley formula (internally consistent with `analytics.js`) rather than encoding a practitioner lookup table (e.g. Tuchscherer/RTS). The config marks this as `contested: true`. When a future PR consumes the module for session planning, consider whether to add a verified practitioner table as a `config/coaching/intensity/rpe-percent-table.json` alongside the formula-based approach, or accept the Epley approximation as "good enough" for prescription. No action until a consumer exists.

- **SafetyClassifierModule: substring matcher is fragile in both directions** `[trust-critical]` — `_matchesAny` uses bidirectional substring: active ⊆ config OR config ⊆ active. This is fragile in two directions: (1) **False negative / paraphrase miss:** "chest tightness" does NOT match the red config signal "chest pain/pressure/tightness (esp. with radiation...)" because neither is a contiguous substring of the other — it falls into `unmatchedSignals` and defaults to green. Similarly "I think I'm going to pass out" won't match "fainting or near-fainting". (2) **False positive via short-token bleed:** a short active signal that is a substring of a config phrase can match the wrong tier — e.g. `"pain"` matches green's `"no pain"` (config ⊆ active direction). In practice this is currently rescued because dangerous tokens also appear in red phrases (`"pain"` ⊆ `"sharp or tearing pain"`, so red wins), but that is incidental, not by design. An unrecognized-but-serious symptom defaulting to green contradicts `on_uncertainty: "err toward caution"`. Before any consumer wires this into the coach surface, replace the bidirectional substring approach with a more robust strategy (e.g. token-level keyword matching for red-flag terms; unmatched signals with no green evidence → default yellow) so the module cannot silently classify a red-flag paraphrase as green/proceed. `[trust-critical]` because the safety layer is the most conservative module and is intended to override all others.

- **FatigueAssessmentModule: INPUT_DIRECTION hardcoded in module** `[housekeeping]` — `INPUT_DIRECTION` (sleep/motivation/hrv = positive, soreness/stress/recent_load = inverted) is a module-level constant rather than derived from config; `fatigue.rules.json` carries no directionality field. The `?? 1` fallback silently defaults any future new readiness input to positive directionality. If directionality ever becomes config-driven, the module will need an update. No action until the config gains a directionality field.

- **SafetyClassifierModule: `getTrafficLightState` result unchecked before property access** `[housekeeping]` — in `classifyTrafficLight`, `stateRecord = getTrafficLightState(state)` is called with a state value that is always `'green'|'yellow'|'red'` (from config tiers or the `?? 'green'` default), so the null path is not reachable today. However, if `safety.rules.json` is ever edited to rename or remove a tier, `stateRecord` would be null and `stateRecord.meaning`/`.action` would throw. For a module whose job is safe degradation, a null-guard fallback (e.g. `?? { meaning: '', action: '' }`) is in character. Deferrable until the config is touched or a consumer wires the module. (Reviewer note, PR #734.)

- **Coaching modules: cached objects returned by reference (not frozen)** `[housekeeping]` — `getExerciseById`/`getAllExercises` (ExerciseLookupModule), `getVolumeLandmarks`/`getAllVolumeLandmarks` (VolumeModule), and `getScenario`/`getIncrements`/`getPlateauRule` (ProgressionRulesModule) return shared cached object references. Callers that mutate a returned object corrupt the in-process cache for all subsequent callers. No write-capable consumers exist today, so not a live risk. When a future consumer mutates returned objects, consider `Object.freeze` at index-load time in each module's `_load()`. Top-level arrays are already `.slice()`-copied defensively; the freeze gap is one level deeper (object properties).

- ✅ **ConfidenceModule: ask-vs-act decision engine (roadmap PR 19, shipped)** — `services/confidenceModule.js` synthesizes signals from prior Brian-layer modules into a single confidence score driving the `act` / `act_with_caveat` / `ask` recommendation. Four weighted dimensions: completeness (0.30), recency (0.25), consistency (0.25), selfReportReliability (0.20). Tiers: ≥75=high→act, ≥45=moderate→act_with_caveat, <45=low→ask. Safety inversion: an active safety flag escalates action one step toward ask (`safetyInverted:true`). Six machine-readable `CAVEAT_KEYS` exported for the LLM to word. 42 tests. No runtime consumer yet. Unblocks roadmap PR 20 (BrianOrchestrator).
  - **`safetyFlags.active` shape contract has no upstream producer** `[trust-critical]` — `scoreConfidence` reads `safetyFlags?.active` (line 197) expecting `{ active: string[] }`, but `classifyTrafficLight()` (`services/safetyClassifierModule.js`) returns `{ state, meaning, action, matchedSignals, unmatchedSignals, confidence }` — no `.active` field. The `{ active: string[] }` shape is only ever constructed in test fixtures. When a future orchestrator (PR 20) wires these modules together, passing the raw `classifyTrafficLight()` result as `safetyFlags` will silently make `hasSafetyFlag = false` and safety inversion will never fire. Fix before PR 20: define and document the expected `safetyFlags` input shape in `confidenceModule.js`, and confirm that the orchestrator maps `matchedSignals → active` (or equivalent) before calling `scoreConfidence`. (Reviewer note PR #742 claude[bot].)
  - **Declining trend emits no caveat at high confidence** `[correctness]` — `_trendBaseScore('declining')` returns 60, and the `LOW_TREND_CONFIDENCE` caveat fires only when `consistency.score < 40`. With `trendConfidence:'high'` the multiplier is 1.0 → `consistency.score = 60` ≥ 40 → no caveat. Combined with a full data fixture, `confidenceScore ≈ 90`, `action: 'act'`, `caveats: []`. The declining trend is carried structurally in `dimensions.consistency.trend` but nothing guarantees the LLM sees it; the lifter can receive an `act` recommendation on a declining lift with zero indication in the caveats. Consider adding a `DECLINING_TREND` caveat key (or lowering the `LOW_TREND_CONFIDENCE` threshold to ~55 so declining+high still triggers it). Deferred to the PR 20 orchestrator or a targeted ConfidenceModule patch. (Reviewer note PR #742 claude[bot].)
  - **`_scoreSelfReport` penalty skipped when `inputsUsed = 0`** `[correctness]` — The guard `inputsUsed > 0` in line 156 was meant to prevent a zero-denominator ratio, but it also suppresses the 0.7 penalty when `inputsAbsent > 0 && inputsUsed = 0` — the worst-case "no inputs provided, all absent" returns a full base score. A `readinessResult` with `confidence:'high'`, `inputsUsed:0`, `inputsAbsent:5` scores 100 instead of ~70. Fix: apply the multiplier when `inputsAbsent > 0` regardless of `inputsUsed`. (Reviewer note PR #742 claude[bot].)
  - **`'stalling'` branch in `_trendBaseScore` is dead code** `[housekeeping]` — `trendDetector.detectTrend()` emits only `'improving' | 'flat' | 'declining' | 'noisy' | 'insufficient_data'`. The `trend === 'stalling'` check at line 97 is therefore unreachable. Remove or replace with `'flat'` if the intention was to treat stalling as flat. (Reviewer note PR #742 claude[bot].)
  - **Tier boundary test name does not match fixture** `[housekeeping]` — Test "score at TIER_ACT_MIN (75) is high → act" (`test/confidenceModule.test.js` line 140) uses a fixture that produces `confidenceScore ≈ 83–88` (verified: LIFT_RICH has `daysSinceLastSession:2` → recency=100, not 80). The test comment's own math shows ~82.5. No `assert.equal(r.confidenceScore, 75)` assertion exists. Add a fixture that genuinely produces 75 (the exact boundary) and assert both `tier: 'high'` and `confidenceScore: 75`. (Reviewer note PR #742 claude[bot].)

- **`isCycleBoundary` contract wording: clarify that caller must pass already-incremented maxes before PR 22 wires it** `[housekeeping]` — `isCycleBoundary: true` fires on the first session of the new cycle (`cyclePos === 0`). That session is built from the `trainingMaxes` supplied in the same call — no auto-increment. A naive PR 22 consumer that reads the flag and increments *after* consuming the returned session would run the entire new-cycle opening session on old maxes. The test added in PR #744 pins the contract, but the JSDoc in `intermediateProgramModule.js` ("caller should increment trainingMaxes before the next call") is ambiguous about *which* call counts as "next." Update JSDoc to read: "The boundary session (cyclePos === 0) uses the maxes supplied to this call; pass already-incremented maxes to receive a correctly-loaded cycle-open session." (`services/intermediateProgramModule.js:97`, reviewer note PR #744 claude[bot].)

- **`applyPopulationCaps` intensity unit convention must be pinned before PR 22 wires it** `[housekeeping]` — `intensity_ceiling_percent_1rm` in population configs is stored as an integer percent (e.g. `85` = 85%). If a PR 22 caller passes `intensity_percent_1rm` as a fraction (e.g. `0.85`), the `Math.min(0.85, 85)` cap silently no-ops and no intensity ceiling is applied. Pin the unit convention (integer percent throughout, or add a normalisation step) and add a test asserting the boundary at `1.0` before the periodization engine consumes this. (`services/populationCapsModule.js:81`, reviewer note PR #744 claude[bot].)

- **`isPeakingAppropriate` reads hardcoded sets instead of `peaking.json` config** `[housekeeping]` — `_PEAKING_EXCLUDED_POPS` and `_PEAKING_ELIGIBLE_GOALS` duplicate `excluded_populations`/`eligible_goals` from `peaking.json`; if the owner edits the JSON expecting a behavior change, nothing happens. Refactor to source these from `_loadFile('peaking.json')` so the config stays the single source of truth, matching how `buildBlockPhases`/`getDupSessionTypes` are data-driven. Safe today (both match). (`services/periodizationModule.js:107`, reviewer note PR #745 claude[bot].)

- **`periodizationModule` shallow `Object.freeze` — spread inner arrays before wiring a session-planner consumer** `[housekeeping]` — `_loadFile` uses `Object.freeze(raw)` which is shallow; nested arrays in returned config objects (`rep_range`, `percent_tm_range`, `set_range`) remain shared mutable references. A consumer mutating a returned array would corrupt the cache for all subsequent calls. Fix before a session-planner wires into this module: spread arrays on return in `buildBlockPhases`, `getDupSessionTypes`, and `buildPeakingPhase` (e.g. `rep_range: [...p.rep_range]`). Also in the same pass: document in JSDoc that `buildPeakingPhase` returns a result with empty `prescriptions{}` (not `null`) when every TM is non-finite — consumers must not assume non-empty. Safe today — no runtime consumer yet. (`services/periodizationModule.js`, reviewer notes PR #745 claude[bot].)

- **`detectLiftPlateaus`: `sessions_stalled` always equals `minSessions` (reports window size, not true stall run length)** `[housekeeping]` — `lastN = sessions.slice(-minSessions)` always has exactly `minSessions` entries once the guard passes, so `sessions_stalled: lastN.length` is always equal to `minSessions`. A future enhancement could track the actual consecutive-stall run (e.g. "stalled for 7 sessions when only 3 were required") as a separate field. Defensible documented choice for now; no consumer yet. (Reviewer note PR #738 claude[bot].)

- **`detectLiftPlateaus` warm-up exclusion is `isWarmupNote`-only; `computeExpectedPerformance` also applies `rir >= 4` heuristic** `[housekeeping]` — An untagged heavy feeler set (no warm-up note, but RIR ≥ 4) is excluded from expected-performance calculation by `computeExpectedPerformance` but is still included in the per-session e1RM in `detectLiftPlateaus`. In practice this biases conservatively (could cause a false negative — might miss a real stall but will never invent one). When a future PR touches `detectLiftPlateaus`, add the same `rir >= 4` fallback filter after `isWarmupNote` to align the two components' warm-up definitions. Non-blocking; no runtime consumer yet. (Reviewer note PR #738 claude[bot].)

- **CHANGELOG "Brian PR X" numbering vs roadmap PR numbering** `[housekeeping]` — The CHANGELOG uses sequential "Brian PR X" labels (order of implementation) while the PDF roadmap (`docs/COACH_INTELLIGENCE_ROADMAP.md`) uses its own logical PR numbers 1–24. These sequences diverge: `feat(brian-pr-14)` in the CHANGELOG is VolumeAssessmentModule (roadmap PR 4), while roadmap PR 14 is ExpectedPerformance/Plateau. Flagged as non-blocking by the automated reviewer (PR #736 claude[bot]). To prevent confusion: future CHANGELOG entries should cite the roadmap PR number explicitly, e.g. `feat(coach-pr-6-user-state)`. A retroactive rename of prior CHANGELOG entries is cosmetic-only and low priority.

## Goal management surface — deferred (owner idea 2026-07-12) `[design]`

Not active; do not build without explicit owner promotion. The concrete filing of the PR-B8a "goal EDITING is a later surface" note, scoped after the owner's 2026-07-12 "I currently have no active goals — don't invent them" decision.

- **Goal management surface — a Goals panel for create / edit / pause / remove, plus chat-detected goal suggestions that require an explicit Save / Edit / No confirmation card. Never create goals automatically.** The read/plumbing already exists (`services/athleteGoals.js` reader → `sanitizeAthleteGoals` whitelist → chat voice; empty state = `null` = no goal claims, verified). This item is the *write/edit* half: an owner-facing surface to set and manage goals, and a suggestion flow where Atlas may *detect* a goal from conversation ("I want to hit 225 bench") and *offer* it on an explicit confirmation card — but a goal only ever persists on an explicit owner Save/Edit, never silently. Storage stays the ratified D8 Constraints `goal`-row convention (no schema change) unless a new decision says otherwise. Any real persistence is a `Constraints` **write** → owner-approved, trust-loop-scoped. **Guardrails (hard):** never auto-create, auto-edit, or auto-resume a goal; no fabricated goals/deadlines; a declined suggestion writes nothing. Build only when the owner promotes it.

## Someday / future scope

_Not active queue. Guiding principle: Atlas infers from data and behaviour; it does not interrogate the user._
- Readiness — non-intrusive: primary signal stays the existing objective per-muscle fatigue/recovery status. Subjective input strictly opt-in (a volunteered note or optional post-session rating); Atlas never prompts, never runs a sleep/stress questionnaire, never auto-adjusts a session off a feelings prompt.
- Proactive alerts / nudges — optional, opt-in, not naggy.
- Injury awareness — remember injuries as constraints on programming.
- Nutrition (Fuel) — real macro tracking, eventually diet↔performance correlation. Way down the road.
- Signature Programs / "Train Like..." mode — future themed workout experiences, not active roadmap work. Possible forms: classic bodybuilding-style blocks, strength-athlete-style blocks, approved creator/influencer partnerships, or creator-built 3-week / 5-session / 12-week programs. Guardrails: do not use real person names, likeness, branding, or paid creator programs unless licensed/approved; famous-athlete references must stay "inspired by classic bodybuilding style" unless rights are secured; creator programs require opt-in partnership and explicit permission. Program themes provide structure only — Atlas still owns personalization, load sanity, substitutions, safety constraints, recovery-aware adjustments, and logging trust.
- Minor / if-ever: session search, import-export & backup, light/dark, settings layer. (Multi-user account auth stays out — this is a personal single-owner tool. "Auth stays out" means **no multi-user account system** — no sign-up, accounts, roles, or public login — NOT that the single owner must re-enter a key: the signed single-owner session that keeps Dale logged in (the durable owner session, F04C) is a session-persistence mechanism, not multi-user auth.)

## Strategic direction — deferred brainstorms

These are not engineering items. They are owner-decision hold points: questions to revisit at specific milestones before choosing a direction. Do not act on them; do not add them to the active roadmap. Revisit only when the trigger condition is reached.

- **Conversation-first coach direction — owner-gated Vision decision (2026-07-01):**
  Owner brief: should Atlas intentionally return to being *"an AI coach with capabilities"* (the conversation is the product; surfaces are artifacts the coach shows, not destinations you navigate to) rather than continuing to drift into *"a fitness app with AI"*? Full analysis, six-question review, and aggressive counter-case in [`docs/CONVERSATION_FIRST_DESIGN_REVIEW.md`](docs/CONVERSATION_FIRST_DESIGN_REVIEW.md). Review verdict: **yes, as consolidation toward the already-written Constitution/Vision — not a pivot** — but **engine before surface**: the One-Brain keystones (Intent Router, Scenario Classifier, Session Generator, Constraint Resolver — see "One-Brain Coaching Engine" below) are preconditions, and three things must be kept (buttons-as-intent-shortcuts, a glance affordance, an always-present `✅ logged` acknowledgment). **Owner refinements (2026-07-01):** (a) the non-negotiable filter *"the conversation is the product; every screen supports it, none competes with it"* is now **Invariant I1** (`docs/INVARIANTS.md`), a forward filter on new work; (b) conversation-first **orchestrates** capabilities, it does not replace them (a one-tap log *is* the conversation — not chatbot-only); (c) a **Conversation Contract** milestone precedes the Intent Router — captured as [`docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md`](./docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md). **Owner-reserved (Vision / coaching-philosophy):** do not implement, and do not amend Vision/Constitution, until the owner approves the direction. If approved, the next step is the Conversation Contract, then the One-Brain keystone sequence — not UI code.

- **After workout session authority / substitution state is stable:**
  Brainstorm what must be separated architecturally for Atlas to become a training-intelligence engine that other surfaces (apps, platforms, APIs) could consume. What is tightly coupled today that would need to be a clean, versioned contract?

- **The "Drift Conversation" — future apex proactivity (owner, 2026-07-01), gated on longitudinal cross-domain history:**
  Atlas opens a session with an evidence-based observation after ~weeks of subtle decline (training frequency down + sleep slipped + performance flat) — *notices the pattern, invites context, then adapts* — instead of jumping to conclusions. Full worked example in `docs/CONVERSATION_CONTRACT_V1.md` (Part 5). Explicitly **not a v1 behavior**: requires cross-domain state Atlas does not yet assemble (multi-week training + wearable sleep/HR trends) and sits inside the earned-proactivity policy (rule 4). Revisit once the longitudinal state layer + wearable trend signals exist. Also: the three owner-authored validation scenarios (Recovery Day, Genuine Plateau, Missed-Week Return) in that doc double as **future QA fixtures** for the coach voice — wire them into `/qa` regression once the Brain surfaces (deload, layoff-return, recovery hold) are live.

- **Decade-relationship coaching research — long-horizon guidance only (2026-07-01):**
  The strategic red-team review produced a permanent research source: [`docs/research/coaching-intelligence/source-archive/06-decade-relationship-coaching.md`](docs/research/coaching-intelligence/source-archive/06-decade-relationship-coaching.md) — *"The Ten-Year Athlete"*. It consolidates the red-team findings (as permanent risk patterns), the ten-year coach/athlete relationship analysis (relationship memory, coach promises and receipts, trust dynamics, communication compression with tenure), and 18 distilled Atlas Coaching Principles. **Not an engineering item, not a roadmap item — do not act on it.** Its role is a standing consultation source: future agents must read it (its Part V checklist) **before** designing work that touches coach memory, relationship intelligence, proactivity, coach promises, injury memory, return-after-layoff behavior, decision-outcome learning, personalization, long-term programming, or coach communication (including coachNoteTier evolution). It does not interrupt or resequence the coachNoteTier / conversation-prototype work, and it changes no Vision/Constitution/Invariant text.

- **"The Feel of Atlas" interaction-philosophy research — guidance only (2026-07-02):**
  The owner-commissioned deep-research pass produced a permanent research source: [`docs/research/coaching-intelligence/source-archive/07-the-feel-of-atlas.md`](docs/research/coaching-intelligence/source-archive/07-the-feel-of-atlas.md) — *"The Feel of Atlas"*. It grounds, in HCI/psycholinguistics/coaching/motivation/AI-interaction research, why conversation feels effortless (grounding, alignment, turn-taking), the inferred guided↔freestyle coaching spectrum, the conversational intent model (visible vs hidden intent; infer/ask/challenge/stay-quiet), the rhythm of coaching (Wooden observation data), a 16-state athlete conversation-state model, complete workout conversation models, coaching-trust failure modes (sycophancy, nagging, abandonment), the day-1→year-10 relationship arc, and **100 timeless interaction principles**. **Not an engineering item, not a roadmap item — do not act on it.** Its role is a standing consultation source: future agents must read it **before** designing, changing, or reviewing anything the athlete experiences in conversation — coach voice/verbosity/silence rules, Conversation Contract behavior dials, Intent Router conversational-intent design, celebration/praise/motivation policy, proactivity, error recovery, onboarding feel. Companion to source 06 (06 = the decade's memory/trust architecture; 07 = how each minute of it should feel). It does not interrupt or resequence any active lane and changes no Vision/Constitution/Invariant text.

- **Conversation acceptance-test suite — reference (2026-07-02):** [`docs/product/conversation-suite/README.md`](docs/product/conversation-suite/README.md) — Conversation acceptance-test suite (P-001–P-025 pressure, N-001–N-040 everyday) — the feel-spec for coach voice, logging, plan flow, session finish, substitutions, safety, and conversation UX. Reference pointer only — not a task.

- **After first private beta users:**
  Brainstorm monetization paths: Pro tier, trainer portal, creator programs, paid onboarding, shareable reports, partnerships. No commitment until the owner has real usage data.

- **After 100 users:**
  Reassess product direction: consumer app vs trainer platform vs creator-program engine vs licensing/API layer. Each path has different architecture, business model, and trust-contract implications.

- **After 1,000 users:**
  Reassess partnership and acquisition positioning. Evaluate whether Atlas should expose an engine/API layer and what that requires architecturally, legally, and operationally.

## Parser — unit normalization touches free-text prose (2026-07-04, review note on PR #845) `[polish]`

`normalizeWeightUnits` (the kg→lb / lb·# pre-pass added in PR #845) runs a global replace over the ENTIRE parse input, so a kg/lb/# marker embedded in free-text prose is also rewritten — e.g. a skip note "shoulder felt like 200kg" becomes "…440.9". Correct for structured set tokens; the only edge is if such prose reaches a STORED free-text field (skip notes / notes). Low severity, out of scope for #845 (a targeted fix would need to restrict conversion to set-context tokens, which the downstream parser — not this pre-pass — is what distinguishes). Capture only; revisit if a real note is ever seen corrupted.

## AI overnight full-app test sweep — findings (2026-07-04, owner-requested "test everything, poke at everything")

Five parallel AI QA testers hit the LIVE deployed app across read-only endpoints, parser, coach red-team, write-path/auth guards, and intent-router accuracy. **Core trust guards verified intact**: dry-run proof fields, hard bounds (weight/reps), auth (timing-safe 401s), write_id enforcement, undo rejection/409 paths, optional-tab 503s, and the coach's no-write/no-secret-leak/no-invented-history discipline all PASS. No unintended-write vector, no auth bypass, no stack-trace leak found. Full reports in the session scratchpad (`findings/`).

**FIXED in the sweep PR (branch `claude/ai-web-app-testing-kxptkz`), with tests:**
**DEFERRED — parser** `[trust-critical]` (high-risk `services/workoutTextParser.js`; several are product/schema decisions → owner-gated). Valid gym language silently dropped or mis-logged:
- **kg unit unparseable** — every `kg`/`kgs` variant (`bench 100kg 5/2`, `100 kg`) drops to `needs_clarification`. **Owner decision: unit-storage policy** — store kg as-is (mixed-unit column) or convert to lb? Analytics/e1RM assume lb, so this is a schema/data-integrity decision, not a pure parse fix.
- **`225x5` (single weight×reps) dropped** and **`5x225` (sets×weight) dropped** — ubiquitous shorthand; only the `x`-triple and slash forms parse. Correct semantics for `5x225` is a product decision (5 sets? clarify?).
- **`225 5,5,5` fabricates a phantom `5 lb` set** — comma rep-list mis-parsed into a junk second set with no warning (a *mis-log*, not a drop — most directly violates the trust contract of the parser findings).
- Space-before-unit (`225 lb 5/2`) and `#` unit (`225# 5/2`) dropped while `225lb`/`225lbs` work.
- RPE silently discarded (`225 5 @rpe8` → rir/rpe null); RIR dropped when phrased `5 reps 2 rir`; rep ranges (`8-10`) dropped; semicolon separator dropped (comma/then/and/newline work); "pull ups" (spaced plural) → unknown exercise; superset entries dropped; no sanity bound on the text path (`999999`, `0 0/0` parse clean); glued `2255/2` → 2255 reps.

**DEFERRED — read-endpoint hygiene** `[correctness]` (frontend-caller check needed before changing response shape):
- `/api/search/sessions` **ignores its `q` param entirely** — returns all 96 sessions + a 351 KB payload on every call (search does not search). Check `public/` callers before making it filter.
- Bare response envelope (no `requestId`/`message`) on `/api/exercises/:code`, `/api/history/recent`, `/api/session/:id` (singular) — plural siblings are full.
- Missing-optional-tab inconsistency: `coaching-notes`/`constraints`→200 empty, `deload`→200 default, but `bodyweight/history`→400. Align.
- `/api/session/:id/summary` returns 200 with a fabricated `quality_score:22` for a **nonexistent** session (sibling `/api/sessions/:id` correctly 404s).
- `recommend/next/:code` emits a global `OFFER_DELOAD` block for a zero-history lift `[polish]`.

**DEFERRED — coach** `[correctness]` (owner-gated — coaching philosophy):
- `/api/coach/chat` **invents non-deterministic prescription loads** ("today's plan" bench/leg-press weights + new exercises change on every call for the same data) — contradicts "the engine owns every number." Ground plan prescriptions in engine `plan_state`; **owner decision** on whether the chat voice should prescribe loads at all.

**DEFERRED — intent router (shadow-lane, pre-promotion; `services/intentRouter.js`)** `[product-evidence]` — calibration data for the C3 promotion decision, not prod behavior:
- 4 deterministic `ok:false` classifier failures on short/negated inputs silently drop constraints — **one drops an injury** (`tweaky lower back today`); also `arms and abs`, `what's my best squat`, `no shoulder issues today`.
- `bump the weight to 225` → `log_intent@0.9` (a correction misread as a new log).
- Cardio/step signal recall 40% (`ran 5k`, `walked 12000 steps` → `clarify_intent@0.3`); undo/delete under-route to `clarify` not `modify_workout`; `no squat rack` → `exclude_exercises` not `equipment`. Constraint precision 87% / recall 90%; compound multi-constraint extraction excellent.

## Owner-approved AI live-retest sweep — deferred findings (2026-07-08)

Five simulated conversational sessions against the LIVE deployed app (read-only, `test_mode:true` dry-run preview only, no writes). Finding #1 (coach cross-lift number misattribution) was fixed in the same-day PR that added this section (`services/coach.js` LIFT-IDENTITY RULE + `test/coach.test.js` regression tests). Findings #2–#5 below are deferred, not fixed here.

- **Harness limitation, not a product bug:** headless-Chromium browser automation against the deployed app is currently blocked in the Claude Code remote-execution environment (TLS connection reset mid-handshake through the session's egress proxy; plain HTTPS `fetch`/`curl` to the identical endpoint succeed). The sweep fell back to driving the same read-only/dry-run endpoints directly over HTTPS. This means pure client-DOM behavior — the session-pin, active-session banner, and the localStorage restore-banner flow after a reload — could not be exercised or verified by this sweep. Revisit with either an owner hands-on pass or a future environment where headless Chromium can reach the deployment.

- **Off-plan/unframed lift logged mid-mutation is silently absorbed with no coherence flag** `[correctness]` — during a plan-mutation conversation (e.g. "switch to upper body day"), logging a lift that doesn't correspond to any current plan slot (e.g. a squat variant, on what the plan now frames as an upper-body day) is accepted with zero acknowledgment; the next "what's next?" answer never flags the mismatch. Likely the same scattered-session-state class already flagged as the top active risk in `docs/REMEDIATION_REVIEW_2026-07-08.md`. No trust/write-path risk (nothing mis-saved) — a recap-coherence gap in the coach/state-store layer.

- **Mid-session "how many sets have I done" can undercount** `[correctness]` — asked after logging 2 sets of one exercise + 1 set of another (3 sets total), the chat replied "2 sets" — apparently derived from the count of distinct completed exercises (`plan_completed`) rather than a true set tally. The chat context payload (`current_preview`, `current_plan`, `plan_completed`) carries no cumulative set-count field for the coach to cite. `services/coach.js` / `buildChatSystemPrompt` chat-context contract.

- **Plan-mutation reply coherence is non-deterministic** `[polish]` — repeating the same "switch to upper body day" prompt twice produced one internally coherent reply (clean upper-body-only `propose_plan_edit`) and one internally contradictory reply (narrated "switching to upper body" while the proposed plan still retained a lower-body lift, justified with the old rationale in the same breath). LLM sampling variance on today's `buildChatSystemPrompt` PROPOSE_PLAN_EDIT instructions; no data was written either way.

- **Ambiguous referent after a "what's next?" answer** `[polish]` — a follow-up "how much?" / "how many sets?" right after the coach names the next planned exercise answers about the just-**logged** exercise instead of the just-**named-next** one. Minor conversational-referent-resolution gap in the chat voice, not a numbers-safety issue.
